Run 1 missed one bound: one ask of twenty in `card_in_the_session_that_is_slow_to_read` took 8.4 s, on a machine at load 240 with three timing tests running at once. That case has two busy sessions and no idle one, so no look is taken in it. Run alone three times straight after, at load 170 to 206, its slowest ask was 1.9, 2.0 and 3.9 s, and in run 2 it was 1.9 s. The numbers are the lab's, loopback through an `ssh` forward: they say the looks add little and nothing about a real link.

**For tickets 35, 37 and 40.**

- Nothing here writes a mirror, so no commit and no pass is asked for by a look.
- `Step::Watch` is the step to copy for another read that no card rests on: the connection sends it like `Step::Since` and keeps it out of the lanes.
- Until ticket 37 brings stored history, a far row whose turn ended unseen shows idle by the live list while its session is listed, and is ordered by its start.
- Ticket 40 should count, on the Mini, how many real turns fall outside the `finished` promise.

**Tests.** `agents/src/hermes/gateway/tests/turns.rs` (the model, each acceptance case by name, and `positions_decide_no_card`, which runs every other gateway model test with the looks forced on and forced off); `core/src/sound_alerts/tests/relaunch.rs`; `platform/src/clients/hermes/tests/machines/alerts.rs` (the lab: the machines the app runs, a far gateway behind a throwaway `sshd`, a real core with sound on); `tests/far/looks.rs`; `tests/far_timing.rs` (by hand); `platform/src/hooks/told.rs`.

**Not verified here:** a real far machine (nothing in this ticket contacts the Mac Mini); how often real far turns fall outside the promise; Linux.
## Live token counts of a far session (ticket 35)

> Ticket 60 changed three things said below: the count calls are asked apart from the polls, on a lane of their own; a session's last count is asked until it is answered; and a new session's row and its first count are two commits. See "Count calls on a lane of their own, and what changes in a grown mirror (ticket 60)".

Spec "Phase 2 design", section 13, is the design. This is what was measured, what was chosen and what was built.

**The two sources, measured.** One socket that subscribed to nothing asked both, for every live session, every half second. "Events" is the read a poll makes of a session that works: one call for where its stream is, one for the events Hermes keeps.

| | `session.stats` | `session.usage` events in the poll's read |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Calls it adds to a poll | 1 for each session asked | 0: the read is made anyway |
| Bytes, lab, Hermes 0.22.5 | 492 to 541 a reply | 0 more. The read itself is 2.5 KB for a short turn and 40 KB for two finished turns |
| Time, lab, 0.22.5 (loopback, load 5 to 7) | 0.7 to 0.9 ms in the middle, 5.9 ms at most | 0 more. The read itself: 1.1 to 2.6 ms in the middle |
| Session started over the local socket, mid-turn | Counts after the first model call: 100 in, 20 out, 0.5 to 1.4 s into the turn | No event until the turn's second step. In the lab's turn of one tool call the first event came with the turn's end, 8 to 10 s later |
| A scheduled job's session, while it runs | Zeros for the whole run | No event at all |
| A session of a messaging platform | The agent's counters, as for a local session (`gateway/session_authority.py`, `agent`), where the gateway holds the agent | No event: Hermes publishes it for a local viewer's turn alone (`gateway/run_turn_progress.py`, `local_viewer_turn`) |
| Mac Mini, Hermes 0.23.1, over the real link | Answered for every live session asked, 15 of 15 in two runs. 458 bytes a reply. 9 ms in the middle and 199 ms at most on 2026-11-18 (34 and 114 ms on 2026-11-16, with this Mac at a load near 300). The reply has the same 23 fields as on 0.22.5. Every reply was zeros: no session worked during either run | 0 of 15 sessions had a `session.usage` event among their last 128 events, a Telegram session with 97 events kept among them. The read itself: 20 ms and 3.3 KB in the middle for a scheduled job's 5 events, 274 ms and 125 KB for the Telegram session |

The lab's runs are `evidence/35/measure/` in the scratch folder; `tests/fixtures/hermes/gateway/session-stats.json` holds one reply of each kind as recorded.

**The choice: `session.stats`.** It costs one small call for each session that works, and it is the only one of the two that answers for the sessions a far machine runs. The events cost nothing and are published only for a turn started over the gateway's own local socket, which is not how a far machine's sessions start, and even there they trail the count by a whole step. Neither source gives a scheduled job's count: Hermes runs a job on an agent the gateway does not hold, so `session.stats` answers zeros for it. Such a session shows no tokens until its history is read (ticket 37). No subscription is held for any of this.

**Which sessions are asked, and when** (`gateway/sample.rs`). A poll of a far gateway asks each session its own list says is working, in the round that follows the list, beside the reads of that session's events. A session that started or worked at the poll before and is listed as something else now (it went idle, waits on a question, or starts again) is asked once more, since a turn's last call adds to the count as the turn ends. Nothing else is asked: not an idle session, not one that only waits, not one that only starts, and no session of a gateway of this Mac, whose database is read where it is. A session that starts has an agent that has not begun, so its count cannot move; the Mac Mini lists one session as starting for days, and asking it at every poll cost a call a second for nothing, which is why starting alone is not asked. So the cost is one call for each session that works in each poll. A reply of zeros, a reply with a count that is no whole number and a call that failed give no sample; the session is asked again at the next poll while it works. A gateway that does not know the call answers an error each time, which costs that one call and nothing else. No card is decided by a sample: the model's cards are the same with the calls answered, failed or never made (`a_count_decides_no_card`).

**The path.** `Gateway::listing()` carries the samples of the newest poll that counted, with the live list they came with. The follower queues both for the machine's mirror writer in one write, with the time the poll came back and the route number its socket was opened under, as ticket 34's rows are.

- `mirror::live` (`agents/src/hermes/mirror/live.rs`) keeps a sample in `hud_live`: the session, the four counts, when it was read. It drops one that is all zeros, one that totals no more than the one kept, one read no later than the one kept, and one of a session the mirror has no row for. It is one statement, and the only one in the crates that writes `hud_live`; it writes no other table. `only_the_live_write_writes_hud_live_and_it_writes_nothing_else` reads every source file of the crates to hold both.
- The reader (`hermes/transcript.rs`) puts `hud_live`'s total on every record of the session as `Record::live_total`, for a mirror alone. The total is part of the session's change fingerprint, so a new sample reads that session again and no other. The records count what they counted without it.
- Core (`source_db/scan.rs`) keeps the largest it was given on the log, `Log::live_total`, and on the saved head. Neither saves the field for a log without one, so every other client's saved logs are byte for byte what they were.
- The row and the session page show `LogHead::shown_tokens`: the larger of the ledger's total and `live_total` (`session_totals`, `log_session`). Nothing else reads it. The snapshot's totals, statistics, the cost buckets, quota history, plan value, a day's totals on the Sessions page, and the session as statistics name it (`topSessions`) are the ledger's alone.

**Why it may not enter the ledger.** `transcript/running_totals.rs` adds a running total by record key and never takes one back. A count of 100 from the gateway under one key, then the far database's rows of 60 and 40 under two other keys, would leave 200. The tests in `core/src/tests/hermes_live.rs` hold the three orders the two can arrive in (samples first, rows first, rows between two samples), each ending with 100 in the ledger, 100 in statistics and 100 on the row.

**Spacing.** Ticket 34's writer tells core after every commit, and that asks for a pass with no spacing. A count moves with every call a session's model answers, so the writer does not commit counts as they come (`platform/src/clients/hermes/mirror.rs`, `SPACE`):

- a machine's counts are committed at most once in 2 s, which is what the file watcher's events are held to;
- the newest sample of each session is the one written, every profile's mirror at that one time, with one word to core that names every file that changed;
- a count that waits is written when its time comes whether or not another poll follows, so the last count of a turn lands;
- a poll whose counts did not move commits nothing and tells nothing, and a count the mirror already holds is not written again after a relaunch;
- a new session's row is still written at once, and where its count's time has come the two are one commit;
- a count read under a route number that is no longer the machine's is dropped, when it is queued, while it waits and in the step that commits.

**What it does not do.**

- A scheduled job's far session shows no count while it runs (above).
- A session whose agent Hermes loaded anew counts from nothing again. Its samples are lower than the one kept and are dropped until they pass it, so the row stands still for a while and never goes down.
- The count is the gateway's four counters: input, output, cache read, cache write. The far database's rows can say more for the same session (the lab's sessions had a usage row of an auxiliary call that the agent's counters leave out). The row then shows the ledger's total, which is the larger.
- A turn that begins and ends between two polls of a session that was idle is never asked. Its tokens arrive with its history.
- `usage` by kind on the row and the session page stays the ledger's. Only `tokens` is the larger number.

**Timing** (spec section 10), in the lab at the app's own pace, with the gateway's hints withheld and no file watcher. Two runs of each at the code as merged, on 2026-11-18 (`evidence/35/timing/`). A miss fails a test, and none missed.

| Case | Bound | Measured: least, middle, most |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `count_when_the_gateway_was_busy` (new): from Hermes saying more to the row showing it, 20 runs | 6 s | 447, 1022, 1757 ms and 438, 1046, 1746 ms (load 11 to 22). One `session.stats` call in each poll: 50 calls in 51 polls |
| `row_when_the_gateway_was_busy_and_counts_move` (new): a new row while four sessions' counts grow every 150 ms, 20 runs | 6 s | 58, 542, 944 ms (load 104) and 40, 536, 846 ms (load 10) |
| `row_when_the_gateway_was_busy` (ticket 34's) | 6 s | 35, 558, 966 ms (load 43) and 41, 531, 842 ms (load 15) |
| `row_when_it_was_idle` (ticket 34's) | 15 s | 415, 5410, 9926 ms (load 41) and 424, 5453, 9913 ms (load 6) |

A count that moves again within the spacing waits it out: in the lab test of one turn, three counts in a row each showed 2.0 s after Hermes said them. The longest a count can take is one poll (up to 1 s), the spacing (2 s) and one pass.

Ticket 30's suite, `far_timing`, with **the count calls and ticket 33's turn looks both on**: three runs on 2026-11-18 after ticket 33 merged (`evidence/35/after-33/`), 10 of 10 in each. The tenth test is ticket 33's, with ten idle sessions beside one that works. In a round the reads the cards wait on go out first, then the looks, then the count calls.

| Test | Bound | Run 1 (load 71 falling to 8) | Run 2 (load 8 to 10) | Run 3 (load 10 to 12) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `card_in_a_busy_session` | 19 of 20 within 4 s, none over 6 s | 2, 475, 790 ms | 23, 528, 826 | 3, 462, 793 |
| `card_beside_a_showing_card` | the same | 19, 525, 822 | 14, 528, 829 | 14, 526, 812 |
| `card_with_five_busy_sessions` | the same | 25, 524, 825 | 27, 521, 832 | 18, 515, 819 |
| `card_behind_a_slow_replay` | the same | 15, 518, 820 | 17, 514, 825 | 3, 439, 784 |
| `card_in_the_session_that_is_slow_to_read` | the same | 83, 1277, 2011 | 76, 1074, 1894 | 80, 1333, 1994 |
| `card_with_ten_idle_sessions` (ticket 33's) | the same | 3, 473, 794 | 37, 520, 826 | 3, 466, 797 |
| `card_in_an_idle_listed_session` | 13 s | 669 | 834 | 743 |

Before ticket 33 merged the suite had also passed 9 of 9 three times with the count calls alone (`evidence/35/timing/`, load 4 to 13, and once at 125 to 280). The numbers are the lab's, over loopback through an `ssh` forward.

**The Mac Mini**, read only, at the code as merged (`evidence/35/mini/receipt.txt`, 2026-11-18, this Mac at a load of 5 to 8).

- Free disk, checked first: 12.6 GB.
- Both gateways `Ready` on Hermes 0.23.1. One serves `default`, `scout`, `skeptic` and `trader`, the other `reports`. 25 live sessions listed (24 idle, 1 starting), no request waiting, no card.
- Polls over the real link with the count step in the code: the first gateway 130 polls, 21 ms in the middle and 836 ms at most, 4 calls each (its four profiles' lists); the second 102 polls, 11 ms in the middle and 120 ms at most, 1 call each. All within 1 s. No poll made a `session.stats` call, since no session worked.
- The measurement asked 12 sessions both ways (the table above). Every frame written, by method: `profiles.list` 2, `session.active_list` 622, `session.events.since` 24, `session.stats` 12. Nothing that subscribes or answers.
- All five `config.yaml` hashes are the same before and after. 68 commands ran there, each with its status in the receipt: 41 ended 0, and 27 ended 2, each an `identify` of a profile home whose gateway is another home's, where nobody listens.
- `ssh -O check` on each of the three control paths fails, no `ssh` of the run is left, and the control folder is gone. Nicky's own `ssh` files are as they were.
- **What the Mini did not show:** a count over zero. `session.stats` answered, with the fields the model reads, and said zeros every time, because Hermes held no agent for any session that was asked. A far session's count moving on a real machine is still to be seen, and so is whether a Telegram session's agent is the one the call reads on 0.23.1; that is from Hermes's source.
- Two earlier tries, on 2026-11-16 with this Mac at a load near 300, are kept beside the receipt. In the first the link did not come up within its 60 s and nothing ran on the Mini. The second connected and measured, a Telegram session among its three, and then could not open its last link, so it has no hashes from after. It is the run that found a session listed as starting for days being asked at every poll (140 calls, each poll 72 ms in the middle against 28 for the other gateway), which is why a session that only starts is not asked.

**Tests.** `agents/src/hermes/gateway/tests/samples.rs` (the model), `mirror/live_tests.rs` (the write, and the source scan), `transcript_tests.rs` (the reader), `core/src/tests/hermes_live.rs` (the ledger, statistics, cost and quota, a relaunch), `platform/src/clients/hermes/mirror/tests/live.rs` (the writer and its spacing, with a real core), `tests/machines/counts.rs` (the lab), `tests/far_rows_timing.rs` (the bounds, by hand), `tests/far_mini_counts.rs` (the measurement on a real far machine, by hand).

**Not verified here:**

- A far session's count moving on a real machine (above).
- A gateway where `session.stats` is slow. The call sits in the poll, with no lane of its own as a slow read of events has, so it would hold the polls up for as long as it takes. It took 9 ms in the middle on the Mini.
- The app's own window process showing a far count. The lab runs the machines, the writer and a real core, not the installed app.
- Linux and Windows. The new code has no OS conditions.

## Far history, kept in step (ticket 37)

Spec "Phase 2 design", section 14, is the design; this is what was built, what was measured on the Mac Mini, and what tickets 39 and 40 must know.

| File | Holds |
| --- | --- |
| `agents/src/hermes/far_history/refresh.rs` | What the refresh decides, pure: whose each read of a tick is (`turn`), when a sweep is overdue, and which sessions a far `active_sessions.json` says the desktop app holds. |
| `agents/src/hermes/mirror/history.rs` | The durable write, pure: `durable` (a session's row and every usage row, `hud_rows` set to durable and to its fingerprint), `held`, `reads` and `note` (`hud_reads`), `stored` (the fingerprint each session was written with). |
| `platform/src/clients/hermes/history.rs`, `history/plan.rs` | `History`: one for each far machine, its thread and its ticks. `Plan`: the interval, the reads a tick may make, the page, the sweep time. `Ages`: what the machines list says. |
| `platform/src/clients/hermes/history/tick.rs`, `tick/sweep.rs` | One home's part of a tick: the first queue, the two sweeps, the read of sessions by id, and the tick's one write of the mirror's ages. |
| `platform/src/clients/hermes/mirror/history.rs` | The writer's part: `Writer::history` (a batch as one transaction, waited for), the live sessions each poll listed (`Writer::first`), and when a poll last found one in a turn (`Writer::working`, ticket 61). |
| `platform/src/clients/hermes/machines/make.rs` | A far machine is made with its followers, its mirror writer and its refresh, and ended with all three (`Far::stop`). |

**Whose it is.** The refresh is the machine's, like its mirror writer: `Book::make` starts it with the machine and `Far::stop` ends it, which is what retiring the machine and turning client hooks off both call. A route change that leaves the machine an owner leaves the refresh where it was, with its sweep's place, on the same mirror. It runs on a thread of its own, shares the machine's link with the polls and nothing else, and holds no model's lock and no lock of core's.

**A tick** reads every Hermes home of the machine in turn, eight reads of each at most (`Plan::reads`). A read is one request of the history reader of ticket 36 (a page of fingerprints, sessions by id, the held file). Three kinds of work wait:

1. *The first queue*: one read of which sessions the desktop app holds, then the sessions that are live (what the newest poll listed, which passes through the writer), just ended (what left that list since the last tick), or held, by id, twenty-five a read. What a tick could not get to is first in the next, so a queue that is never empty still serves every session in turn.
2. *The recent sweep*: fingerprints of the sessions active in the last seven days, a page of 500 rows at a time, a whole pass in each tick where the reads allow it.
3. *The whole sweep*: fingerprints of the whole table, a page at a time by rowid, beginning anew at its end.

A fingerprint is held against the one its session was last written with (`hud_rows.print`), and only a session whose fingerprint differs is fetched, before the next page is read.

**Fairness** (`refresh::turn`). Every fourth read of a tick is the whole sweep's, however full the first queue is, so the sweep advances in every tick. A whole sweep must finish within 24 hours (`Plan::sweep_within`): once the last one that finished is older than that, or none ever did, every read is the sweep's until it is done. So the first sync of a machine is the sweep alone, and its live sessions' stored counts wait for it. A tick with nothing else to do still gives the sweep one read, and then rests.

**Writing.** Everything goes through the machine's one mirror writer, as a `Write` on its queue that the refresh waits for.

- A session's row and every one of its usage rows are one transaction, with `hud_rows` set to durable, to the time the read began and to the fingerprint the far reader gave. The usage rows are replaced, never added to.
- **A row the live list made is replaced in place, by `UPDATE`**, and no `sessions` row is ever deleted, so every row keeps its rowid (ticket 54's rule: the reader knows a new session by a rowid above every one it read).
- A session the mirror holds from a read that began no earlier is left as it is: a batch that comes back late is dropped, session by session.
- A batch is committed only while its route number, taken before its read began, is still the machine's (`Routes::under`), and not at all once the machine is retired. A batch the writer did not take is not taken for done: its sessions wait, and the sweep's place stays.
- `hud_live` is never touched. Core is told of a commit only where a row it reads changed, so a tick that found nothing new asks for no pass. The tick's own word (`hud_reads`, `hud_rows.held`) is one more commit for each home, of which core is not told.

**What a batch never does to the ledger.** The reader makes of a mirror written this way what it makes of the far database itself, record for record and key for key (`a_sessions_durable_rows_are_written_whole_and_the_reader_reads_them_as_the_far_database`), so usage is counted as for a session of this Mac: by running totals for each key of model, provider, base URL, billing mode and task. A row the live list made has no count and adds nothing; the same batch again adds nothing; a session that grew adds what it grew by (`core/src/tests/hermes_history.rs`).

**One reader slot.** Every read asks the machine's one slot, which the book keeps for as long as client hooks are on (`Book::slots`), so a machine that is retired and added again has the slot it had. The refresh never makes one. `History::stop` tells the slots the machine was retired, so the next read there probes first.

**A page that takes the far side too long** (the reader's own deadline, or this side's wait) is asked for at half the size from the next tick on, down to ten rows, until that sweep ends. A slow database slows the sweep and does not stop it.

**A session that cannot be read** (more than 500 usage rows, or too large for one read) keeps its old rows, is remembered with the fingerprint it had, and is asked for again only when that changes. The memory is the refresh's own and is lost at a relaunch, after which each such session is asked for once more. A session the mirror refuses is set aside the same way (ticket 61, below).

**The two ages.** `hud_reads` holds, for each mirror, when its profile was last read at all (`contact_ms`), where its whole sweep goes on from (`sweep_cursor`), and when the sweep under way began and the last one that finished did. The machines list shows, for a connected far machine, the newest contact with any of its profiles and the oldest of their last whole sweeps, which is none until every profile that has a database was swept whole once. The whole-check time moves only when a sweep finishes. A relaunch reads the sweep's place from the mirror and goes on from there.

**A machine with no history reader** (no `python3` with `sqlite3`, or a SQLite older than 3.43) stays connected, with its cards, and says so in its status's note; it is asked again at the slow pace. A home with no `state.db` is skipped and has no age.

**The interval.** A tick every 60 s while a far session is in a turn, a whole sweep is overdue, or nothing could be read at all; every 5 minutes otherwise, however many idle sessions the machine lists (ticket 61, below). The first tick comes 10 s after the machine is followed.

### Measured on the Mac Mini, and what was chosen

Read only, on 2026-11-18, over the real link, with both gateways running on Hermes 0.23.1 (`evidence/37/mini/receipt.txt`). The Mini's `default` database is 2.99 GB with 2,204 sessions and 2,167 usage rows; `reports` is 112 MB with 290 and 348; `scout`, `skeptic` and `trader` hold three or four sessions each. Two runs, the second after ticket 55's change to how the reader ends; the numbers are the second's, with this Mac at a load of 4 to 5 on 12 cores. Every read of both ended with status 0 and nothing on standard error.

| Read | `default` (2.99 GB): bytes back, time of 5 or 6 | `reports` (112 MB): bytes back, time |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Probe (opens nothing) | 39 B, 198 to 376 ms | 39 B, 203 to 264 ms |
| A page of fingerprints, 500 rows | 54,740 B, 209 to 313 ms | 39,715 B (all 290 rows), 208 to 282 ms |
| The same page, sessions active in the last 7 days | 264 B (2 of 500), 199 to 246 ms | 6,257 B (44 of 290), 215 to 232 ms |
| 25 sessions with their usage rows | 16,153 B, 211 to 250 ms | 15,154 B, 207 to 235 ms |
| The held file | 395 B, 204 to 225 ms | 397 B, 192 to 257 ms |

- **A read costs a quarter of a second whatever it reads and however large the database is.** Nearly all of it is the far Python starting and the round trip; the three small profiles took the same 160 to 480 ms. The first run, with this Mac at a load of 62, measured 215 to 439 ms for the same reads (`evidence/37/mini-run-1-before-the-rebase-onto-55/`).
- **The first sync of all five profiles**, 2,504 sessions, took 299 reads in 12 ticks run back to back: 75 s, a tick 5.8 to 7.2 s, in both runs. At the app's pace, a tick a minute while a sweep is overdue, it takes about 12 minutes. Every mirror then held exactly the rows counted on the Mini, before and after: 2,204 and 2,167, 290 and 348, 4 and 2, 3 and 2, 3 and 2. No session was skipped.
- **A tick once in step** made 22 to 24 reads over the five homes in 5.6 to 7.2 s.
- **The polls did not slow down.** One poll of each gateway a second: 17 ms in the middle and 71 ms at most with no history read (100 polls), 17 ms and 549 ms while history reads ran back to back (459 polls), every one within the second a poll has. In the first run: 21 and 273 ms without, 18 and 268 ms with. No card can be made on the Mini without sending something, so the time from asking to showing was measured in the lab, below.
- **A new row with the synced mirrors behind it**: core read the five mirrors in 23 passes and listed 2,504 sessions; a row then written by the mirror writer was listed at the first pass, 161 ms after its commit (a test build, at a load of 4). In the writer's own test, a mirror of 2,000 stored sessions lists a new row at the first pass with no pass after it.
- **No intentional write, and no write to `state.db` or to a `-wal` that was there.** The `config.yaml` of all five profiles hashes the same before and after. Every file in the five homes has the name and the size it had; no `-wal` or `-shm` file is new. The five `state.db-wal` files kept their size and have a later time of change. That is Hermes's own doing (corrected by ticket 61; this line said the cause was not found): each gateway writes its log about once a minute, and the times in the four listings of the two runs sit on a 60-second beat, one second apart by profile, that began before the first read and went on between the runs with no HUD connected. A few more files of Hermes's own have a later time and the same size: `auth.lock`, `gateway_state.json` (two homes), `quota_cache.json`, `kanban.db-shm`, `profiles/reports/state.db` (a gateway folding its log in) and, in the first run, `channel_directory.json`. What the reader itself may touch is in "Leftovers after the verifier" below. No far reader was left, and no HUD `ssh` master.

**The choices.**

- *The interval stays at the starting point*: 60 s while a far session is in a turn or a sweep is overdue, 5 minutes otherwise ("in a turn" and not "listed" since ticket 61). A tick is about 24 short processes on the Mini, 6 s of one core's work spread over a few seconds, and the polls beside it are not slowed. A shorter interval would buy nothing the live counts of ticket 35 do not already show, and a longer one would show a scheduled job's tokens later, which no live count shows at all.
- *The sweep time stays at 24 hours.* It is a limit and not the pace: a whole sweep of the Mini's largest table is five pages, done in three ticks, so in fifteen minutes at the slow pace. At 500 rows a page and two sweep reads a tick, a table a hundred times that size is still swept within 24 hours at the slow pace.
- *Eight reads of a home in a tick, 500 rows a page, 25 sessions a read.* The page and the batch are the reader's own bounds from ticket 36, and both answers were far inside the output bound (55 KB and 16 KB of 1 MiB).
